Verified pre-rebuild backups for the doctor safety stack (Phase 7.2, layer 3).\n2\t//!\n3\t//! Before the doctor ever rebuilds SQLite from JSONL (a last-resort, destructive\n4\t//! operation), it snapshots the full DB family plus the JSONL authority into a\n5\t//! per-run recovery directory and records a SHA-256 hash for every copied file.\n6\t//! Because each backup carries verifiable hashes:\n7\t//!\n8\t//! * a rebuild that fails post-verification can be rolled back to a known-good state\n9\t//!   automatically (`restore_run`), and\n10\t//! * an operator can list past runs (`bf doctor --runs`) and restore any of them by\n11\t//!   id or `latest` (`bf doctor --restore <run-id|latest>`).\n12\t//!\n13\t//! The recovery directory lives at `.beads/recovery/`. Each run is a subdirectory\n14\t//! named for its run id (a filesystem-safe UTC timestamp) containing byte-for-byte\n15\t//! copies of the backed-up files and a `manifest.json` describing them.\n16\t\n17\tuse anyhow::{anyhow, Context, Result};\n18\tuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\n19\tuse sha2::{Digest, Sha256};\n20\tuse std::path::{Path, PathBuf};\n21\t\n22\t/// Name of the recovery directory under `.beads/`.\n23\tpub const RECOVERY_DIR: &str = \"recovery\";\n24\t\n25\t/// Name of the per-run manifest file.\n26\tconst MANIFEST_NAME: &str = \"manifest.json\";\n27\t\n28\t/// Marker written when a rebuild fails its post-verification (layer 5). its verification hash.\n33\t#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq)]\n34\tpub struct BackedUpFile {\n35\t    /// File name as it appears in `.beads/` (e.g. `beads.db`, `beads.db-wal`).\n36\t    pub name: String,\n37\t    /// Lowercase hex SHA-256 of the file's bytes at backup time.\n38\t    pub sha256: String,\n39\t    /// Size in bytes at backup time.\n40\t    pub bytes: u64,\n41\t}\n42\t\n43\t/// Describes a single recovery run: what was backed up, when, and why.\n44\t#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq)]\n45\tpub struct BackupManifest {\n46\t    /// Filesystem-safe run identifier (also the subdirectory name).\n47\t    pub run_id: String,\n48\t    /// RFC3339 creation timestamp.\n49\t    pub created_at: String,\n50\t    /// Human-readable reason (e.g. \"pre-rebuild\").\n51\t    pub reason: String,\n52\t    /// Files captured in this run, each with a verification hash.\n53\t    pub files: Vec<BackedUpFile>,\n54\t}\n55\t\n56\t/// Compute the lowercase hex SHA-256 of a file's contents.\n57\tpub fn hash_file(path: &Path) -> Result<String> {\n58\t    let bytes = std::fs::read(path)\n59\t        .with_context(|| format!(\"Failed to read {} for hashing\", path.display()))?;\n60\t    let digest = Sha256::digest(&bytes);\n61\t    Ok(format!(\"{:x}\", digest))\n62\t}\n63\t\n64\t/// Path to the recovery directory for a given `.beads` directory.\n65\tpub fn recovery_dir(beads_dir: &Path) -> PathBuf {\n66\t    beads_dir.join(RECOVERY_DIR)\n67\t}\n68\t\n69\t/// Generate a filesystem-safe run id from the current time.\n70\t///\n71\t/// Millisecond resolution keeps two rebuilds in the same second from colliding.\n72\tfn generate_run_id() -> String {\n73\t    let now = chrono::Utc::now();\n74\t    format!(\n75\t        \"run-{}-{:03}\",\n76\t        now.format(\"%Y%m%dT%H%M%S\"),\n77\t        now.timestamp_subsec_millis()\n78\t    )\n79\t}\n80\t\n81\t/// Create a verified backup of `files` into a fresh run directory.\n82\t///\n83\t/// Each source path that exists is copied into `.beads/recovery/<run-id>/` under\n84\t/// its file name, and its SHA-256 hash is recorded in the run manifest. Sources\n85\t/// that do not exist (e.g. a missing `-wal`/`-shm` sidecar) are skipped silently.\n86\t///\n87\t/// Returns the manifest describing the run.\n88\tpub fn create_backup(beads_dir: &Path, Verified pre-rebuild backups for the doctor safety stack (Phase 7.2, layer 3).\n//!\n//! Before the doctor ever rebuilds SQLite from JSONL (a last-resort, destructive\n//! operation), it snapshots the full DB family plus the JSONL authority into a\n//! per-run recovery directory and records a SHA-256 hash for every copied file.\n//! Because each backup carries verifiable hashes:\n//!\n//! * a rebuild that fails post-verification can be rolled back to a known-good state\n//!   automatically (`restore_run`), and\n//! * an operator can list past runs (`bf doctor --runs`) and restore any of them by\n//!   id or `latest` (`bf doctor --restore <run-id|latest>`).\n//!\n//! The recovery directory lives at `.beads/recovery/`. Each run is a subdirectory\n//! named for its run id (a filesystem-safe UTC timestamp) containing byte-for-byte\n//! copies of the backed-up files and a `manifest.json` describing them.\n\nuse anyhow::{anyhow, Context, Result};\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse sha2::{Digest, Sha256};\nuse std::path::{Path, PathBuf};\n\n/// Name of the recovery directory under `.beads/`.\npub const RECOVERY_DIR: &str = \"recovery\";\n\n/// Name of the per-run manifest file.\nconst MANIFEST_NAME: &str = \"manifest.json\";\n\n/// Marker written when a rebuild fails its post-verification (layer 5). While it\n/// exists, further rebuilds refuse unless `--allow-repeated-repair` is passed.\nconst REPAIR_FAILED_MARKER: &str = \"repair-failed.marker\";\n\n/// One backed-up file and its verification hash.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct BackedUpFile {\n    /// File name as it appears in `.beads/` (e.g. `beads.db`, `beads.db-wal`).\n    pub name: String,\n    /// Lowercase hex SHA-256 of the file's bytes at backup time.\n    pub sha256: String,\n    /// Size in bytes at backup time.\n    pub bytes: u64,\n}\n\n/// Describes a single recovery run: what was backed up, when, and why.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct BackupManifest {\n    /// Filesystem-safe run identifier (also the subdirectory name).\n    pub run_id: String,\n    /// RFC3339 creation timestamp.\n    pub created_at: String,\n    /// Human-readable reason (e.g. \"pre-rebuild\").\n    pub reason: String,\n    /// Files captured in this run, each with a verification hash.\n    pub files: Vec<BackedUpFile>,\n}\n\n/// Compute the lowercase hex SHA-256 of a file's contents.\npub fn hash_file(path: &Path) -> Result<String> {\n    let bytes = std::fs::read(path)\n        .with_context(|| format!(\"Failed to read {} for hashing\", path.display()))?;\n    let digest = Sha256::digest(&bytes);\n    Ok(format!(\"{:x}\", digest))\n}\n\n/// Path to the recovery directory for a given `.beads` directory.\npub fn recovery_dir(beads_dir: &Path) -> PathBuf {\n    beads_dir.join(RECOVERY_DIR)\n}\n\n/// Generate a filesystem-safe run id from the current time.\n///\n/// Millisecond resolution keeps two rebuilds in the same second from colliding.\nfn generate_run_id() -> String {\n    let now = chrono::Utc::now();\n    format!(\n        \"run-{}-{:03}\",\n        now.format(\"%Y%m%dT%H%M%S\"),\n        now.timestamp_subsec_millis()\n    )\n}\n\n/// Create a verified backup of `files` into a fresh run directory.\n///\n/// Each source path that exists is copied into `.beads/recovery/<run-id>/` under\n/// its file name, and its SHA-256 hash is recorded in the run manifest. Sources\n/// that do not exist (e.g. a missing `-wal`/`-shm` sidecar) are skipped silently.\n///\n/// Returns the manifest describing the run.\npub fn create_backup(beads_dir: &Path, files: &[PathBuf], reason: &str) -> Result<BackupManifest> {\n    let root = recovery_dir(beads_dir);\n    std::fs::create_dir_all(&root)\n        .with_context(|| format!(\"Failed to create recovery dir {}\", root.display()))?;\n\n    let run_id = generate_run_id();\n    let run_dir = root.join(&run_id);\n    std::fs::create_dir_all(&run_dir)\n        .with_context(|| format!(\"Failed to create run dir {}\", run_dir.display()))?;\n\n    let mut backed_up = Vec::new();\n    for src in files {\n        if !src.exists() {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let name = src\n            .file_name()\n            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!(\"Backup source has no file name: {}\", src.display()))?\n            .to_string_lossy()\n            .into_owned();\n        let dest = run_dir.join(&name);\n        std::fs::copy(src, &dest)\n            .with_context(|| format!(\"Failed to copy {} to backup\", src.display()))?;\n\n        // Hash the copy we just wrote; if the copy is faithful its hash matches the\n        // source, and this is the exact byte stream a later restore reads back.\n        let sha256 = hash_file(&dest)?;\n        let bytes = std::fs::metadata(&dest)?.len();\n        backed_up.push(BackedUpFile {\n            name,\n            sha256,\n            bytes,\n        });\n    }\n\n    let manifest = BackupManifest {\n        run_id: run_id.clone(),\n        created_at: chrono::Utc::now().to_rfc3339(),\n        reason: reason.to_string(),\n        files: backed_up,\n    };\n\n    let manifest_json = serde_json::to_string_pretty(&manifest)?;\n    std::fs::write(run_dir.join(MANIFEST_NAME), manifest_json)\n        .with_context(|| format!(\"Failed to write manifest for run {}\", run_id))?;\n\n    Ok(manifest)\n}\n\n/// Load the manifest for a single run directory.\nfn load_manifest(run_dir: &Path) -> Result<BackupManifest> {\n    let manifest_path = run_dir.join(MANIFEST_NAME);\n    let json = std::fs::read_to_string(&manifest_path)\n        .with_context(|| format!(\"Failed to read manifest {}\", manifest_path.display()))?;\n    let manifest: BackupManifest = serde_json::from_str(&json)\n        .with_context(|| format!(\"Failed to parse manifest {}\", manifest_path.display()))?;\n    Ok(manifest)\n}\n\n/// List all recovery runs, newest first.\n///\n/// Run ids are lexicographically ordered by construction (timestamp prefix), so a\n/// reverse sort yields newest-first without parsing timestamps.\npub fn list_runs(beads_dir: &Path) -> Result<Vec<BackupManifest>> {\n    let root = recovery_dir(beads_dir);\n    if !root.exists() {\n        return Ok(Vec::new());\n    }\n\n    let mut runs = Vec::new();\n    for entry in std::fs::read_dir(&root)? {\n        let entry = entry?;\n        if !entry.file_type()?.is_dir() {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let run_dir = entry.path();\n        if !run_dir.join(MANIFEST_NAME).exists() {\n            continue;\n        }\n        match load_manifest(&run_dir) {\n            Ok(m) => runs.push(m),\n            Err(_) => continue, // skip unreadable/partial runs rather than failing the listing\n        }\n    }\n\n    runs.sort_by(|a, b| b.run_id.cmp(&a.run_id));\n    Ok(runs)\n}\n\n/// Resolve a run reference (\"latest\" or an explicit run id) to a manifest.\npub fn resolve_run(beads_dir: &Path, run_ref: &str) -> Result<BackupManifest> {\n    if run_ref == \"latest\" {\n        return list_runs(beads_dir)?\n            .into_iter()\n            .next()\n            .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!(\"No recovery runs found to restore\"));\n    }\n    let run_dir = recovery_dir(beads_dir).join(run_ref);\n    if !run_dir.exists() {\n        return Err(anyhow!(\"Recovery run '{}' not found\", run_ref));\n    }\n    load_manifest(&run_dir)\n}\n\n/// Verify that every file in a run still matches its recorded hash.\n///\n/// Returns `Ok(())` when all hashes match; otherwise an error naming the first\n/// file that failed verification (a corrupt or truncated backup must never be\n/// silently restored over a live database).\npub fn verify_run(beads_dir: &Path, run_id: &str) -> Result<()> {\n    let run_dir = recovery_dir(beads_dir).join(run_id);\n    let manifest = load_manifest(&run_dir)?;\n    for f in &manifest.files {\n        let path = run_dir.join(&f.name);\n        if !path.exists() {\n            return Err(anyhow!(\n                \"Backup file {} missing from run {}\",\n                f.name,\n                run_id\n            ));\n        }\n        let actual = hash_file(&path)?;\n        if actual != f.sha256 {\n            return Err(anyhow!(\n                \"Backup file {} in run {} failed hash verification (expected {}, got {})\",\n                f.name,\n                run_id,\n                f.sha256,\n                actual\n            ));\n        }\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Restore a run's files back into `.beads/`, after verifying every hash.\n///\n/// The DB family (`beads.db` and any `-wal`/`-shm` sidecars) present in the backup\n/// replaces the live copies. To avoid a stale sidecar from the *current* (failed)\n/// state resurrecting after restore, any live `-wal`/`-shm` not captured in the\n/// backup is removed first.\npub fn restore_run(beads_dir: &Path, run_ref: &str) -> Result<BackupManifest> {\n    let manifest = resolve_run(beads_dir, run_ref)?;\n    verify_run(beads_dir, &manifest.run_id)?;\n\n    let run_dir = recovery_dir(beads_dir).join(&manifest.run_id);\n    let backed_up_names: std::collections::HashSet<&str> =\n        manifest.files.iter().map(|f| f.name.as_str()).collect();\n\n    // Clear live SQLite sidecars that are not part of the backup; leaving a stale\n    // WAL/SHM alongside a restored .db can corrupt reads.\n    for sidecar in [\"-wal\", \"-shm\"] {\n        for f in &manifest.files {\n            if f.name.ends_with(\".db\") {\n                let live_sidecar = beads_dir.join(format!(\"{}{}\", f.name, sidecar));\n                let sidecar_name = format!(\"{}{}\", f.name, sidecar);\n                if live_sidecar.exists() && !backed_up_names.contains(sidecar_name.as_str()) {\n                    let _ = std::fs::remove_file(&live_sidecar);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    for f in &manifest.files {\n        let src = run_dir.join(&f.name);\n        let dest = beads_dir.join(&f.name);\n        std::fs::copy(&src, &dest)\n            .with_context(|| format!(\"Failed to restore {} from run {}\", f.name, manifest.run_id))?;\n    }\n\n    Ok(manifest)\n}\n\n// ---- Repeat-failure marker (layer 5) ----\n\n/// Path marker.\npub fn repair_failed_marker_exists(beads_dir: &Path) -> bool {\n    marker_path(beads_dir).exists()\n}\n\n/// Write the repeat-failure marker, recording which run's backup was restored.\npub fn write_repair_failed_marker(beads_dir: &Path, detail: &str) -> Result<()> {\n    let root = recovery_dir(beads_dir);\n    std::fs::create_dir_all(&root)?;\n    let body = format!(\n        \"rebuild post-verification failed at {}\\n{}\\n\",\n        chrono::Utc::now().to_rfc3339(),\n        detail\n    );\n    std::fs::write(marker_path(beads_dir), body)?;\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Remove the repeat-failure marker (called after a successful rebuild).\npub fn clear_repair_failed_marker(beads_dir: &Path) -> Result<()> {\n    let path = marker_path(beads_dir);\n    if path.exists() {\n        std::fs::remove_file(&path)?;\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use tempfile::TempDir;\n\n    fn write(path: &Path, contents: &[u8]) {\n These are stale 'blocked' beads that should be 'open' but were never\n144\t    // transitioned when their last blocker closed. Independent of db_ok/jsonl_ok since\n145\t    // it only needs the sqlite db to be openable.\n146\t    let stale_blocked_ids = check_stale_blocked_statuses(&db_path)?;\n147\t    if !stale_blocked_ids.is_empty() {\n148\t        issues.push(format!(\n149\t            \"{} bead(s) stuck in 'blocked' status despite having no active blockers \\\n150\t             -- run `bf update <id> --status open` on each to unblock them: {}\",\n151\t            stale_blocked_ids.len(),\n152\t            stale_blocked_ids.join(\", \")\n153\t        ));\n154\t    }\n155\t    result.stale_blocked_ids = stale_blocked_ids;\n156\t\n157\t    // Check for NULL values stored in NOT NULL columns (bf-3hm5h). Independent of\n158\t    // db_ok/jsonl_ok since it only needs the sqlite db to be openable, and it is\n159\t    // precisely the kind of low-level corruption that other checks (which read\n160\t    // through the storage layer) would otherwise have crashed on.\n161\t    let null_not_null = check_null_not_null(&db_path)?;\n162\t    if !null_not_null.is_empty() {\n163\t        let total: usize = null_not_null.iter().map(|v| v.count).sum();\n164\t        let detail = null_not_null\n165\t            .iter()\n166\t            .map(|v| format!(\"{}.{} ({})\", v.table, v.column, v.count))\n167\t            .collect::<Vec<_>>()\n168\t            .join(\", \");\n169\t        issues.push(format!(\n170\t            \"{} NULL value(s) in NOT NULL column(s) [{}] -- run `bf doctor --fix-schema` \\\n171\t             to repair in place\",\n172\t            total, detail\n173\t        ));\n174\t    }\n175\t    result.null_not_null = null_not_null;\n176\t\n177\t    result.db_ok = db_ok;\n178\t    result.jsonl_ok = jsonl_ok;\n179\t    result.issues = issues;\n180\t\n181\t    Ok(result)\n182\t}\n183\t\n184\t/// Find beads whose status is 'blocked' but have no active (non-terminal) blockers.\n185\t/// These are stale 'blocked' beads that should be 'open' but were never transitioned\n186\t/// when their last blocker closed (bf-5id).\n187\tfn check_stale_blocked_statuses(db_path: &Path) -> Result<Vec<String>> {\n188\t    let conn = Connection::open(db_path)?;\n189\t    conn.busy_timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))?;\n190\t\n191\t    // Find beads where:\n192\t    // 1. status = 'blocked'\n193\t    // 2. NOT deleted\n194\t    // 3. COUNT of active blockers (non-terminal status) = 0\n195\t    let mut stmt = conn.prepare(\n196\t        r#\"\n197\t        SELECT i.id\n198\t        FROM issues i\n199\t        WHERE i.status = 'blocked'\n200\t        AND i.deleted_at IS NULL\n201\t        AND (\n202\t            SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT d.depends_on_id)\n203\t            FROM dependencies d\n204\t            INNER JOIN issues blocker ON blocker.id = d.depends_on_id\n205\t            WHERE d.issue_id = i.id\n206\t            AND d.type IN ('blocks', 'parent-child', 'conditional-blocks', 'waits-for')\n207\t            AND blocker.status NOT IN ('closed', 'tombstone', 'done', 'completed')\n208\t        ) = 0\n209\t        ORDER BY i.id\n210\t        \"#\n211\t    )?;\n212\t\n213\t    let ids = stmt\n214\t        .query_map([], |row| row.get::<_, String>(0))?\n215\t        .collect::<rusqlite::Result<Vec<_>>>()?;\n216\t    Ok(ids)\n217\t}\n218\t\n219\t/// Find beads whose status is a `TERMINAL_STATUS_ALIASES` value (e.g. \"completed\",\n220\t/// \"done\") rather than the canonical \"closed\"/\"tombstone\". These beads are functionally\n221\t/// terminal (see `Status::is_terminal()`) but weren't actually closed, so `bf show`/`bf\n222\t/// list` still displays them as active and `closed_at`/`close_reason` are never set.\n223\tfn check_pseudo_terminal_statuses(db_path: &Path) -> Result<Vec<String>> {\n224\t    let conn = Connection::open(db_path)?;\n225\t    conn.busy_timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))?;\n226\t    let placeholders = crate::model::TERMINAL_STATUS_ALIASES\n227\t        .iter()\n228\t        .map(|s| format!(\"'{s}'\"))\n229\t        .collect::<Vec<_>>()\n230\t        .join(\", \");\n231\t    let query = format!(\n232\t        \"SELECT id FROM issues WHERE LOWER(status) IN ({placeholders}) AND deleted_at IS NULL ORDER BY id\"\n233\t    );\n234\t    let mut stmt = conn.prepare(&query)?;\n235\t    let ids = stmt\n236\t        .query_map([], |row| row.get::<_, String>(0))?\n237\t        .collect::<rusqlite::Result<Vec<_>>>()?;\n238\t    Ok(ids)\n239\t}\n240\t\n241\t/// List the user tables in the database (excluding SQLite internal tables).\n242\tfn user_tables(conn: &Connection) -> Result<Vec<String>> {\n243\t    let mut stmt = conn.prepare(\n244\t        \"SELECT name FROM sqlite_master WHERE type = 'table' AND name NOT LIKE 'sqlite_%' ORDER BY name\",\n245\t    )?;\n246\t    let tables = stmt\n247\t        .query_map([], |row| row.get::<_, String>(0))?\n248\t        .collect::<rusqlite::Result<Vec<_>>>()?;\n249\t    Ok(tables)\n250\t}\n251\t\n252\t/// A NOT NULL column, discovered by introspecting the live schema.\n253\tstruct NotNullColumn {\n254\t    table: String,\n255\t    column: String,\n256\t    decl_type: String,\n257\t}\n258\t\n259\t/// Introspect every user table via `PRAGMA table_info` and return the columns the\n260\t/// schema declares NOT NULL. Driving off the live schema (rather than a hardcoded\n261\t/// list) keeps the detector and fixer correct as the schema evolves -- this is the\n262\t/// \"generally\" in \"NULL-in-NOT-NULL rows generally\" (bf-3hm5h).\n263\tfn not_null_columns(conn: &Connection) -> Result<Vec<NotNullColumn>> {\n264\t    let mut cols = Vec::new();\n265\t    for table in user_tables(conn)? {\n266\t        // PRAGMA table_info columns: cid, name, type, notnull, dflt_value, pk\n267\t        let mut stmt = conn.prepare(&format!(\"PRAGMA table_info(\\\"{}\\\")\", table.replace('\"', \"\\\"\\\"\")))?;\n268\t        let rows = stmt\n269\t            .query_map([], |row| {\n270\t                let name: String = row.get(1)?;\n271\t                let decl_type: String = row.get(2)?;\n272\t                let notnull: i64 = row.get(3)?;\n273\t                Ok((name, decl_type, notnull))\n274\t            })?\n275\t            .collect::<rusqlite::Result<Vec<_>>>()?;\n276\t        for (name, decl_type, notnull) in rows {\n277\t            if notnull == 1 {\n278\t                cols.push(NotNullColumn {\n279\t                    table: table.clone(),\n280\t                    column: name,\n281\t                    decl_type,\n282\t                });\n283\t            }\n284\t        }\n285\t    }\n286\t    Ok(cols)\n287\t}\n288\t\n289\t/// Quote a SQLite identifier (table or column name) for safe interpolation.\n290\tfn quote_ident(name: &str) -> String {\n291\t    format!(\"\\\"{}\\\"\", name.replace('\"', \"\\\"\\\"\"))\n292\t}\n293\t\n294\t/// Detect rows storing NULL in a NOT NULL column (bf-3hm5h).\n295\t///\n296\t/// The schema forbids this, but corrupted databases have contained it -- most\n297\t/// notably a NULL `created_at`/`updated_at` that turned every `bf list`/`bf flush`\n298\t/// into a fatal `InvalidColumnType` crash. Reads are now NULL-tolerant; this\n299\t/// detector surfaces the underlying rows so `fix_null_not_null` can repair them.\n300\tfn check_null_not_null(db_path: &Path) -> Result<Vec<NullNotNullViolation>> {\n301\t    let conn = Connection::open(db_path)?;\n302\t    conn.busy_timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))?;\n303\t\n304\t    let mut violations = Vec::new();\n305\t    for col in not_null_columns(&conn)? {\n306\t        // NOTE: test `typeof(col) = 'null'`, NOT `col IS NULL`. SQLite's query planner\n307\t        // assumes a NOT NULL column never holds NULL and folds `col IS NULL` to a\n308\t        // constant FALSE -- so `IS NULL` can *never* surface this corruption. `typeof()`\n309\t        // forces per-row evaluation and reports 'null' for a stored NULL regardless of\n310\t        // the column's declared constraint.\n311\t        let count: i64 = conn.query_row(\n312\t            &format!(\n313\t                \"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM {} WHERE typeof({}) = 'null'\",\n314\t                quote_ident(&col.table),\n315\t                quote_ident(&col.column)\n316\t            ),\n317\t            [],\n318\t            |row| row.get(0),\n319\t        )?;\n320\t        if count > 0 {\n321\t            violations.push(NullNotNullViolation {\n322\t                table: col.table,\n323\t                column: col.column,\n324\t                decl_type: col.decl_type,\n325\t                count: count as usize,\n326\t            });\n327\t        }\n328\t    }\n329\t    Ok(violations)\n330\t}\n331\t\n332\t/// Repair rows that store NULL in a NOT NULL column, in place (bf-3hm5h).\n333\t///\n334\t/// This is a non-destructive fixer -- unlike `repair`, it does not rebuild from\n335\t/// JSONL, so it works even when JSONL is absent or itself suspect, and it never\n336\t/// touches valid rows. Each NULL is replaced with a type-appropriate sentinel:\n337\t///\n338\t/// * `DATETIME` columns -> the Unix epoch (`1970-01-01T00:00:00+00:00`), matching\n339\t///   the value NULL-tolerant reads already substitute, so a fixed row round-trips\n340\t///   identically to how it was already being displayed.\n341\t/// * `INTEGER`/`REAL`/numeric columns -> `0`.\n342\t/// * everything else (TEXT/BLOB) -> the empty string, matching the schema's\n343\t///   `NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` convention for text columns.\n344\t///\n345\t/// Returns the total number of column values updated.\n346\tpub fn fix_null_not_null(workspace_dir: &Path) -> Result<usize> {\n347\t    let beads_dir = find_beads_dir(workspace_dir)\n348\t        .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!(\"No .beads directory found in {}\", workspace_dir.display()))?;\n349\t    let metadata = load_metadata(&beads_dir)?;\n350\t    let db_path = beads_dir.join(&metadata.database);\n351\t\n352\t    let violations = check_null_not_null(&db_path)?;\n353\t    if violations.is_empty() {\n354\t        return Ok(0);\n355\t    }\n356\t\n357\t    let conn = Connection::open(&db_path)?;\n358\t    conn.busy_timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))?;\n359\t\n360\t    let mut fixed = 0usize;\n361\t    for v in &violations {\n362\t        let upper = v.decl_type.to_uppercase();\n363\t        // SQLite type affinity: DATE/TIME columns first (they must not be caught by\n364\t        // the INT affinity rule below), then numeric, then everything else.\n365\t        let replacement = if upper.contains(\"DATE\") || upper.contains(\"TIME\") {\n366\t            \"'1970-01-01T00:00:00+00:00'\".to_string()\n367\t        } else if upper.contains(\"INT\")\n368\t            || upper.contains(\"REAL\")\n369\t            || upper.contains(\"FLOA\")\n370\t            || upper.contains(\"DOUB\")\n371\t            || upper.contains(\"NUM\")\n372\t            || upper.contains(\"DEC\")\n373\t        {\n374\t            \"0\".to_string()\n375\t        } else {\n376\t            \"''\".to_string()\n377\t        };\n378\t\n379\t        // `typeof(col) = 'null'`, NOT `col IS NULL`: the planner folds `IS NULL` on a\n380\t        // NOT NULL column to FALSE, so an `IS NULL` UPDATE would silently match zero\n381\t        // rows and \"repair\" nothing. See check_null_not_null for the full rationale.\n382\t        let updated = conn.execute(\n383\t            &format!(\n384\t                \"UPDATE {} SET {} = {} WHERE typeof({}) = 'null'\",\n385\t                quote_ident(&v.table),\n386\t                quote_ident(&v.column),\n387\t                replacement,\n388\t                quote_ident(&v.column)\n389\t            ),\n390\t            [],\n391\t        )?;\n392\t        fixed += updated;\n393\t    }\n394\t\n395\t    Ok(fixed)\n396\t}\n397\t\n398\t/// Check database integrity.\n399\tfn check_database(db_path: &Path) -> Result<(usize, bool)> {\n400\t    let conn = Connection::open(db_path)?;\n401\t    conn.busy_timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))?;\n402\t\n403\t    // Apply schema if database is new (no tables yet)\n404\t    let needs_schema: bool = conn\n405\t        .query_row(\n406\t            \"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table' AND name='issues'\",\n407\t            [],\n408\t            |row| row.get::<_, i64>(0),\n409\t        )\n410\t        .map(|n| n == 0)\n411\t        .unwrap_or(true);\n412\t    if needs_schema {\n413\t        crate::storage::schema::apply_schema(&conn)?;\n414\t    }\n415\t\n416\t    // Run PRAGMA integrity_check\n417\t    let integrity_result: String =\n418\t        conn.query_row(\"PRAGMA integrity_check\", [], |row| row.get(0))?;\n419\t\n420\t    let integrity_ok = integrity_result == \"ok\";\n421\t\n422\t    // Count issues\n423\t    let count: i64 = conn.query_row(\n424\t        \"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM issues WHERE deleted_at IS NULL\",\n425\t        [],\n426\t        |row| row.get(0),\n427\t    )?;\n428\t\n429\t    Ok((count as usize, integrity_ok))\n430\t}\n431\t\n432\t/// Check JSONL file validity.\n433\tfn check_jsonl(jsonl_path: &Path) -> Result<(usize, bool)> {\n434\t    if !jsonl_path.exists() {\n435\t        return Ok((0, true)); // Empty workspace is valid\n436\t    }\n437\t\n438\t    let mut count = 0;\n439\t    let mut valid = true;\n440\t\n441\t    match stream_issues(jsonl_path) {\n442\t        Ok(iter) => {\n443\t            for result in iter {\n444\t                match result {\n445\t                    Ok(_) => count += 1,\n446\t                    Err(e) => {\n447\t                        valid = false;\n448\t                        eprintln!(\"Invalid JSONL line: {}\", e);\n449\t                    }\n450\t                }\n451\t            }\n452\t        }\n453\t        Err(e) => {\n454\t            return Err(anyhow!(\"Failed to read JSONL file: {}\", e));\n455\t        }\n456\t    }\n457\t\n458\t    Ok((count, valid))\n459\t}\n460\t\n461\t/// Consistency drift details.\n462\t#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]\n463\tstruct ConsistencyDrift {\n464\t    /// Bead IDs that exist in SQLite but not in JSONL\n465\t    missing_in_jsonl: Vec<String>,\n466\t    /// Bead IDs that exist in JSONL but not in SQLite\n467\t    missing_in_sqlite: Vec<String>,\n468\t    /// Bead IDs where content_hash differs between JSONL and SQLite\n469\t    hash_mismatch: Vec<String>,\n470\t}\n471\t\n472\t/// Check consistency between database and JSONL using content_hash.\n473\t///\n474\t/// Compares each bead's content_hash between JSONL and SQLite to detect drift.\n475\tfn check_consistency_with_hash(db_path: &Path, jsonl_path: &Path) -> Result<ConsistencyDrift> {\n476\t    use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};\n477\t\n478\t    let storage = Storage::open(db_path)?;\n479\t\n480\t    // Build a map of all SQLite issues with their content_hash\n481\t    let sqlite_issues: HashMap<String, String> = storage\n482\t        .list_all_issues()?\n483\t        .into_iter()\n484\t        .map(|issue| {\n485\t            let hash = issue.content_hash();\n486\t            (issue.id, hash)\n487\t        })\n488\t        .collect();\n489\t\n490\t    let mut drift = ConsistencyDrift::default();\n491\t    let mut jsonl_seen: HashSet<String> = HashSet::new();\n492\t\n493\t    // Stream JSONL and compare, tracking seen IDs\n494\t    if jsonl_path.exists() {\n495\t        let iter = stream_issues(jsonl_path)?;\n496\t        for result in iter {\n497\t            let jsonl_issue = result?;\n498\t            let jsonl_hash = jsonl_issue.content_hash();\n499\t            let bead_id = jsonl_issue.id.clone();\n500\t            jsonl_seen.insert(bead_id.clone());\n501\t\n502\t            match sqlite_issues.get(&bead_id) {\n503\t                Some(sqlite_hash) => {\n504\t                    if sqlite_hash != &jsonl_hash {\n505\t                        drift.hash_mismatch.push(bead_id);\n506\t                    }\n507\t                }\n508\t                None => {\n509\t                    drift.missing_in_sqlite.push(bead_id);\n510\t                }\n511\t            }\n512\t        }\n513\t    }\n514\t\n515\t    // Find beads in SQLite but not in JSONL (using tracked seen IDs)\n516\t    for bead_id in sqlite_issues.keys() {\n517\t        if !jsonl_seen.contains(bead_id) {\n518\t            drift.missing_in_jsonl.push(bead_id.clone());\n519\t        }\n520\t    }\n521\t\n522\t    Ok(drift)\n523\t}\n524\t\n525\t/// Count unflushed beads (db-only or db-newer beads not yet flushed to JSONL).\n526\t///\n527\t/// Returns the count of beads that exist in SQLite but have been marked as dirty\n528\t/// (modified or created since the last flush to JSONL).\n529\tpub fn count_unflushed(db_path: &Path) -> Result<usize> {\n530\t    use rusqlite::Connection;\n531\t\n532\t    let conn = Connection::open(db_path)?;\n533\t    conn.busy_timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))?;\n534\t\n535\t    // Check if dirty_issues table exists\n536\t    let table_exists: i64 = conn.query_row(\n537\t        \"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table' AND name='dirty_issues'\",\n538\t        [],\n539\t        |row| row.get(0),\n540\t    )?;\n541\t\n542\t    if table_exists == 0 {\n543\t        return Ok(0);\n544\t    }\n545\t\n546\t    let count: i64 = conn.query_row(\"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM dirty_issues\", [], |row| row.get(0))?;\n547\t\n548\t    Ok(count as usize)\n549\t}\n550\t\n551\t/// Get IDs of unflushed beads.\n552\t///\n553\t/// Returns a list of bead IDs that exist in SQLite but have been marked as dirty\n554\t/// (modified or created since the last flush to JSONL).\n555\tfn get_unflushed_ids(db_path: &Path) -> Result<Vec<String>> {\n556\t    use rusqlite::Connection;\n557\t\n558\t    let conn = Connection::open(db_path)?;\n559\t    conn.busy_timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))?;\n560\t\n561\t    // Check if dirty_issues table exists\n562\t    let table_exists: i64 = conn.query_row(\n563\t        \"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table' AND name='dirty_issues'\",\n564\t        [],\n565\t        |row| row.get(0),\n566\t    )?;\n567\t\n568\t    if table_exists == 0 {\n569\t        return Ok(Vec::new());\n570\t    }\n571\t\n572\t    let mut stmt = conn.prepare(\"SELECT issue_id FROM dirty_issues ORDER BY issue_id\")?;\n573\t    let mut rows = stmt.query([])?;\n574\t\n575\t    let mut ids = Vec::new();\n576\t    while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {\n577\t        ids.push(row.get(0)?);\n578\t    }\n579\t\n580\t    Ok(ids)\n581\t}\n582\t\n583\t/// Repair the database by rebuilding from JSONL.\n584\t///\n585\t/// This is the recovery operation when the database is corrupted or missing.\n586\t/// The JSONL file is the authoritative source of truth for repair.\n587\t///\n588\t/// # Arguments\n589\t/// * `workspace_dir` - Path to the workspace root (contains .beads/)\n590\t/// * `flush_first` - If true, flush unflushed beads to JSONL before repairing\n591\t/// * `force` - If true, proceed even with unflushed beads (they will be lost)\n592\t///\n593\t/// # Returns\n594\t/// * `Ok(usize)` - Number of beads imported\n595\tpub fn repair(workspace_dir: &Path, flush_first: bool, force: bool) -> Result<usize> {\n596\t    let beads_dir = find_beads_dir(workspace_dir)\n597\t        .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!(\"No .beads directory found in {}\", workspace_dir.display()))?;\n598\t    let metadata = load_metadata(&beads_dir)?;\n599\t    let db_path = beads_dir.join(&metadata.database);\n600\t    let jsonl_path = beads_dir.join(&metadata.jsonl_export);\n601\t\n602\t    // Check if JSONL exists\n603\t    if !jsonl_path.exists() {\n604\t        return Err(anyhow!(\n605\t            \"Cannot repair: JSONL file not found at {}\",\n606\t            jsonl_path.display()\n607\t        ));\n608\t    }\n609\t\n610\t    // Check for unflushed beads if database exists and is valid\n611\t    // If db is corrupted, we can't detect unflushed beads - proceed with warning\n612\t    let (unflushed_ids, db_corrupted) = if db_path.exists() {\n613\t        match get_unflushed_ids(&db_path) {\n614\t            Ok(ids) => (ids, false),\n615\t            Err(_) => {\n616\t                // Database is corrupted or unreadable\n617\t                // We can't detect unflushed beads, so proceed with a warning\n618\t                if flush_first {\n619\t                    // Cannot flush from a corrupt database\n620\t                    return Err(anyhow!(\n621\t                        \"Cannot flush: database is corrupted and unreadable.\\n\\\n622\t                         Flushing from a corrupt DB would poison the JSONL checkpoint.\\n\\\n623\t                         Unflushed beads cannot be recovered.\\n\\\n624\t                         Remove --flush-first to proceed with repair only.\"\n625\t                    ));\n626\t                }\n627\t                // Proceed without unflushed check (db is unreadable)\n628\t                // No --force needed since we can't detect unflushed beads anyway\n629\t                eprintln!(\"WARNING: Database is corrupted and unreadable.\");\n630\t                eprintln!(\"  Cannot detect unflushed beads - any db-only beads will be lost.\");\n631\t                eprintln!(\"  Proceeding with repair from JSONL...\");\n632\t                (Vec::new(), true)\n633\t            }\n634\t        }\n635\t    } else {\n636\t        // DB doesn't exist - no unflushed beads possible\n637\t        (Vec::new(), false)\n638\t    };\n639\t\n640\t    if !unflushed_ids.is_empty() {\n641\t        if flush_first {\n642\t            // Flush ALL beads to JSONL first (not just dirty ones)\n643\t            // We must export everything because repair will rebuild db from JSONL\n644\t            eprintln!(\n645\t                \"Flushing all beads to JSONL before repair (including {} unflushed)...\",\n646\t                unflushed_ids.len()\n647\t            );\n648\t            let storage = Storage::open(&db_path)?;\n649\t            let flushed = storage.sync_to_jsonl(&jsonl_path, false)?;\n650\t            eprintln!(\"Flushed {} bead(s) to JSONL\", flushed);\n651\t        } else if !force {\n652\t            // Refuse to proceed with unflushed beads\n653\t            return Err(anyhow!(\n654\t                \"Cannot repair: {} unflushed bead(s) exist ({}).\\n\\\n655\t                 Run 'bf doctor --repair --flush-first' to flush before repair,\\n\\\n656\t                 or 'bf doctor --repair --force' to proceed (these beads will be LOST).\\n\\\n657\t                 Unflushed beads: {}\",\n658\t                unflushed_ids.len(),\n659\t                if unflushed_ids.len() <= 5 {\n660\t                    unflushed_ids.iter().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(\", \")\n661\t                } else {\n662\t                    format!(\n663\t                        \"{}, ... and {} more\",\n664\t                        unflushed_ids[..5].join(\", \"),\n665\t                        unflushed_ids.len() - 5\n666\t                    )\n667\t                },\n668\t                unflushed_ids.join(\", \")\n669\t            ));\n670\t        } else {\n671\t            // Force mode: warn but proceed\n672\t            eprintln!(\n673\t                \"WARNING: {} unflushed bead(s) will be LOST: {}\",\n674\t                unflushed_ids.len(),\n675\t                if unflushed_ids.len() <= 5 {\n676\t                    unflushed_ids.iter().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(\", \")\n677\t                } else {\n678\t                    format!(\n679\t                        \"{}, ... and {} more\",\n680\t                        unflushed_ids[..5].join(\", \"),\n681\t                        unflushed_ids.len() - 5\n682\t                    )\n683\t                }\n684\t            );\n685\t            eprintln!(\"Proceeding with repair due to --force flag\");\n686\t        }\n687\t    }\n688\t\n689\t    // Backup existing database if it exists\n690\t    if db_path.exists() {\n691\t        let backup_path = db_path.with_extension(&format!(\n692\t            \"db.backup.{}\",\n693\t            chrono::Utc::now().format(\"%Y%m%d%H%M%S\")\n694\t        ));\n695\t        std::fs::copy(&db_path, &backup_path)?;\n696\t        eprintln!(\"Backed up existing database to {}\", backup_path.display());\n697\t    }\n698\t\n699\t    // Delete old database\n700\t    if db_path.exists() {\n701\t        std::fs::remove_file(&db_path)?;\n702\t    }\n703\t\n704\t    // Create new database and import JSONL\n705\t    let storage = Storage::open(&db_path)?;\n706\t\n707\t    let result = storage.with_immediate_transaction(|tx| {\n708\t        import_jsonl(&jsonl_path, |issue| {\n709\t            Storage::create_issue_tx(tx, &issue)?;\n710\t            Ok(UpsertResult::New)\n711\t        })\n712\t    })?;\n713\t\n714\t    // Rebuild blocked cache\n715\t    storage.rebuild_blocked_cache()?;\n716\t\n717\t    // Clear dirty marks - after rebuild from JSONL, db and JSONL are in sync\n718\t    // (create_issue_tx doesn't mark dirty, but we clear for safety)\n719\t    storage.clear_dirty()?;\n720\t\n721\t    Ok(result.imported)\n722\t}\n723\t\n724\t// ===========================================================================\n725\t// Doctor safety stack (Phase 7.2 — layered repair + beads_rust#394 preservation)\n726\t//\n727\t// `repair_stack` is the user-facing `bf doctor --repair` path. Unlike the\n728\t// low-level `repair` primitive above (which unconditionally rebuilds from JSONL),\n729\t// it walks a layered safety architecture and only reaches the destructive JSONL\n730\t// rebuild as a last resort, always behind a verified restorable backup:\n731\t//\n732\t//   1. Healthy early-return  — a workspace with no corruption/divergence never\n733\t//      rebuilds; only trivial, non-destructive fixers run.\n734\t//   2. Local repair first    — integrity triage + VACUUM/REINDEX/cache rebuild;\n735\t//      if that resolves the problem, the JSONL rebuild is never reached.\n736\t//   3. Verified backups      — SHA-256-hashed copies of the DB family + JSONL into\n737\t//      `.beads/recovery/<run-id>/` before any rebuild; restore-on-failure.\n738\t//   4. JSONL authority preflight — refuse rebuild if the JSONL carries merge\n739\t//      conflict markers or unparseable records (never rebuild from a poisoned\n740\t//      authority).\n741\t//   5. Repeat-failure gate   — a rebuild that fails post-verification writes a\n742\t//      marker; further rebuilds refuse without `--allow-repeated-repair`.\n743\t//   6. Preservation across rebuild (beads_rust#394) — snapshot unflushed dirty\n744\t//      issues (with labels/deps/comments) *and* tombstones before the rebuild,\n745\t//      restore + re-mark them dirty after, and report the preserved count.\n746\t// ===========================================================================\n747\t\n748\t/// Options controlling a layered `repair_stack` run.\n749\t#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]\n750\tpub struct RepairOptions {\n751\t    /// Flush unflushed (dirty) beads to JSONL before rebuild.\n752\t    pub flush_first: bool,\n753\t    /// Discard unflushed dirty beads instead of preserving them across a rebuild.\n754\t    /// Preservation is the default; `force` opts out (the legacy destructive path).\n755\t    pub force: bool,\n756\t    /// Proceed even if a prior rebuild left a repeat-failure marker (layer 5).\n757\t    pub allow_repeated_repair: bool,\n758\t}\n759\t\n760\t/// Structured outcome of a layered `repair_stack` run.\n761\t#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]\n762\tpub struct RepairReport {\n763\t    /// True when the workspace was healthy (or locally repairable) and no JSONL\n764\t    /// rebuild was performed.\n765\t    pub healthy: bool,\n766\t    /// True when a JSONL rebuild was performed.\n767\t    pub rebuilt: bool,\n768\t    /// Beads imported from JSONL during a rebuild (0 if no rebuild).\n769\t    pub imported: usize,\n770\t    /// Unflushed dirty beads snapshotted and restored across the rebuild (layer 6).\n771\t    pub preserved_dirty: usize,\n772\t    /// Non-destructive local fixers that were applied (layer 2).\n773\t    pub local_fixes: Vec<String>,\n774\t    /// Run id of the verified pre-rebuild backup (layer 3), if a rebuild ran.\n775\t    pub backup_run_id: Option<String>,\n776\t    /// Human-readable notes for the CLI summary.\n777\t    pub messages: Vec<String>,\n778\t}\n779\t\n780\t/// Resolve the `.beads` dir + db/jsonl paths for a workspace.\n781\tfn resolve_paths(workspace_dir: &Path) -> Result<(PathBuf, PathBuf, PathBuf)> {\n782\t    let beads_dir = find_beads_dir(workspace_dir)\n783\t        .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!(\"No .beads directory found in {}\", workspace_dir.display()))?;\n784\t    let metadata = load_metadata(&beads_dir)?;\n785\t    let db_path = beads_dir.join(&metadata.database);\n786\t    let jsonl_path = beads_dir.join(&metadata.jsonl_export);\n787\t    Ok((beads_dir, db_path, jsonl_path))\n788\t}\n789\t\n790\t/// The DB family + JSONL authority paths that a pre-rebuild backup captures.\n791\tfn backup_targets(db_path: &Path, jsonl_path: &Path) -> Vec<PathBuf> {\n792\t    let db_str = db_path.to_string_lossy().into_owned();\n793\t    vec![\n794\t        db_path.to_path_buf(),\n795\t        PathBuf::from(format!(\"{}-wal\", db_str)),\n796\t        PathBuf::from(format!(\"{}-shm\", db_str)),\n797\t        jsonl_path.to_path_buf(),\n798\t    ]\n799\t}\n800\t\n801\t/// Layer 4 — JSONL authority preflight.\n802\t///\n803\t/// Refuse to treat the JSONL as authoritative for a rebuild if it contains git\n804\t/// merge-conflict markers or any unparseable record. Rebuilding from a poisoned\n805\t/// authority is exactly how a bad flush or a botched merge becomes permanent.\n806\tpub fn preflight_jsonl(jsonl_path: &Path) -> Result<()> {\n807\t    if !jsonl_path.exists() {\n808\t        return Ok(()); // A fresh/empty workspace has nothing to poison.\n809\t    }\n810\t\n811\t    // Scan raw lines for conflict markers first — these produce the clearest error.\n812\t    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(jsonl_path)\n813\t        .map_err(|e| anyhow!(\"Cannot read JSONL for preflight: {}\", e))?;\n814\t    for (idx, line) in content.lines().enumerate() {\n815\t        if line.starts_with(\"<<<<<<<\")\n816\t            || line.starts_with(\">>>>>>>\")\n817\t            || line == \"=======\"\n818\t            || line.starts_with(\"======= \")\n819\t            || line.starts_with(\"|||||||\")\n820\t        {\n821\t            return Err(anyhow!(\n822\t                \"Refusing to rebuild: JSONL contains a git merge-conflict marker at line {} \\\n823\t                 (\\\"{}\\\"). Resolve the conflict in {} before repairing — rebuilding from a \\\n824\t                 conflicted authority would make the corruption permanent.\",\n825\t                idx + 1,\n826\t                line.chars().take(20).collect::<String>(),\n827\t                jsonl_path.display()\n828\t            ));\n829\t        }\n830\t    }\n831\t\n832\t    // Then confirm every record parses. A single invalid record means the JSONL is\n833\t    // not a trustworthy authority for a full rebuild.\n834\t    let mut invalid = Vec::new();\n835\t    for (idx, result) in stream_issues(jsonl_path)?.enumerate() {\n836\t        if let Err(e) = result {\n837\t            invalid.push(format!(\"line {}: {}\", idx + 1, e));\n838\t            if invalid.len() >= 5 {\n839\t                break;\n840\t            }\n841\t        }\n842\t    }\n843\t    if !invalid.is_empty() {\n844\t        return Err(anyhow!(\n845\t            \"Refusing to rebuild: JSONL contains {} invalid record(s) [{}]. \\\n846\t             Fix or remove the bad lines in {} before repairing.\",\n847\t            invalid.len(),\n848\t            invalid.join(\"; \"),\n849\t            jsonl_path.display()\n850\t        ));\n851\t    }\n852\t\n853\t    Ok(())\n854\t}\n855\t\n856\t/// Layer 2 — non-destructive local repair.\n857\t///\n858\t/// Runs integrity triage and cheap local recovery (VACUUM, REINDEX, blocked-cache\n859\t/// rebuild). Returns the list of fixers applied. Never touches JSONL and never\n860\t/// drops data; safe to run on any openable database.\n861\tfn local_repair(db_path: &Path) -> Result<Vec<String>> {\n862\t    let mut applied = Vec::new();\n863\t    if !db_path.exists() {\n864\t        return Ok(applied);\n865\t    }\n866\t\n867\t    {\n868\t        let conn = Connection::open(db_path)?;\n869\t        conn.busy_timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))?;\n870\t        // VACUUM compacts and can clear certain free-list inconsistencies.\n871\t        conn.execute_batch(\"VACUUM\")?;\n872\t        applied.push(\"VACUUM\".to_string());\n873\t        // REINDEX rebuilds every index from the (authoritative) table rows.\n874\t        conn.execute_batch(\"REINDEX\")?;\n875\t        applied.push(\"REINDEX\".to_string());\n876\t    }\n877\t\n878\t    // Rebuild the derived blocked-issues cache from the live graph.\n879\t    let storage = Storage::open(db_path)?;\n880\t    storage.rebuild_blocked_cache()?;\n881\t    applied.push(\"rebuild-blocked-cache\".to_string());\n882\t\n883\t    Ok(applied)\n884\t}\n885\t\n886\t/// Core rebuild used by the safety stack: replace the DB by importing from JSONL.\n887\t///\n888\t/// Unlike the `repair` primitive, this performs no dirty-bead guard and writes no\n889\t/// ad-hoc `.db.backup.*` file — the layered caller has already taken a verified\n890\t/// recovery backup and captured any dirty state to restore afterwards.\n891\tfn rebuild_db_from_jsonl(db_path: &Path, jsonl_path: &Path) -> Result<usize> {\n892\t    if db_path.exists() {\n893\t        std::fs::remove_file(db_path)?;\n894\t    }\n895\t    // Remove stale SQLite sidecars so the fresh DB starts clean.\n896\t    for sidecar in [\"-wal\", \"-shm\"] {\n897\t        let p = PathBuf::from(format!(\"{}{}\", db_path.to_string_lossy(), sidecar));\n898\t        if p.exists() {\n899\t            let _ = std::fs::remove_file(&p);\n900\t        }\n901\t    }\n902\t\n903\t    let storage = Storage::open(db_path)?;\n904\t    let result = storage.with_immediate_transaction(|tx| {\n905\t        import_jsonl(jsonl_path, |issue| {\n906\t            Storage::create_issue_tx(tx, &issue)?;\n907\t            Ok(UpsertResult::New)\n908\t        })\n909\t    })?;\n910\t    storage.rebuild_blocked_cache()?;\n911\t    storage.clear_dirty()?;\n912\t    Ok(result.imported)\n913\t}\n914\t\n915\t/// Layer 6 (restore half) — re-insert snapshotted dirty beads after a rebuild and\n916\t/// re-mark them dirty. Beads already reconstituted from JSONL are replaced with the\n917\t/// (newer, unflushed) snapshot version so their in-flight edits survive.\n918\t///\n919\t/// Returns the number of beads restored.\n920\tfn restore_dirty_snapshot(db_path: &Path, snapshot: &[Issue]) -> Result<usize> {\n921\t    if snapshot.is_empty() {\n922\t        return Ok(0);\n923\t    }\n924\t    let storage = Storage::open(db_path)?;\n925\t    let now = chrono::Utc::now().to_rfc3339();\n926\t    storage.with_immediate_transaction(|tx| {\n927\t        for issue in snapshot {\n928\t            if Storage::get_issue_tx(tx, &issue.id)?.is_some() {\n929\t                // JSONL carried an older copy — overwrite with the unflushed snapshot.\n930\t                Storage::update_issue_from_json_tx(tx, issue)?;\n931\t            } else {\n932\t                Storage::create_issue_tx(tx, issue)?;\n933\t            }\n934\t            // Re-mark dirty: these beads are still unflushed relative to JSONL.\n935\t            tx.execute(\n936\t                \"INSERT OR REPLACE INTO dirty_issues (issue_id, marked_at) VALUES (?1, ?2)\",\n937\t                rusqlite::params![issue.id, now],\n938\t            )?;\n939\t        }\n940\t        Ok::<_, anyhow::Error>(())\n941\t    })?;\n942\t    Ok(snapshot.len())\n943\t}\n944\t\n945\t/// Layered, safe `bf doctor --repair` (Phase 7.2).\n946\t///\n947\t/// Walks the six-layer safety stack described above. See [`RepairOptions`] /\n948\t/// [`RepairReport`].\n949\tpub fn repair_stack(workspace_dir: &Path, opts: &RepairOptions) -> Result<RepairReport> {\n950\t    let (beads_dir, db_path, jsonl_path) = resolve_paths(workspace_dir)?;\n951\t    let mut report = RepairReport::default();\n952\t\n953\t    if !jsonl_path.exists() {\n954\t        return Err(anyhow!(\n955\t            \"Cannot repair: JSONL file not found at {}\",\n956\t            jsonl_path.display()\n957\t        ));\n958\t    }\n959\t\n960\t    // ---- Layer 5: repeat-failure gate (checked before doing any work) ----\n961\t    if recovery::repair_failed_marker_exists(&beads_dir) && !opts.allow_repeated_repair {\n962\t        return Err(anyhow!(\n963\t            \"Refusing to repair: a previous rebuild failed post-verification and its backup was \\\n964\t             restored (see {}/{}). Investigate before retrying, then pass \\\n965\t             --allow-repeated-repair to override.\",\n966\t            recovery::RECOVERY_DIR,\n967\t            \"repair-failed.marker\"\n968\t        ));\n969\t    }\n970\t\n971\t    // ---- Layer 1: health assessment ----\n972\t    let health = check(workspace_dir)?;\n973\t\n974\t    // A rebuild is only justified by genuine corruption or JSONL↔DB divergence.\n975\t    // Unflushed (db-only) beads are NOT a rebuild trigger — that is a flush concern,\n976\t    // and rebuilding for them would risk exactly the data we must preserve. Invalid\n977\t    // JSONL counts as unhealthy so the flow reaches the layer-4 preflight, which then\n978\t    // refuses (never rebuild from a poisoned authority) rather than silently passing.\n979\t    let needs_rebuild = !health.db_ok\n980\t        || !health.jsonl_ok\n981\t        || !health.missing_in_sqlite.is_empty()\n982\t        || !health.hash_mismatch.is_empty();\n983\t\n984\t    // ---- Layer 2: local repair first (always safe, never drops data) ----\n985\t    if db_path.exists() {\n986\t        match local_repair(&db_path) {\n987\t            Ok(fixes) => report.local_fixes = fixes,\n988\t            Err(e) => report\n989\t                .messages\n990\t                .push(format!(\"Local repair partially failed: {}\", e)),\n991\t        }\n992\t    }\n993\t\n994\t    // Re-assess after local repair: it may have resolved integrity issues, making a\n995\t    // rebuild unnecessary.\n996\t    let post_local = check(workspace_dir)?;\n997\t    let still_needs_rebuild = !post_local.db_ok\n998\t        || !post_local.jsonl_ok\n999\t        || !post_local.missing_in_sqlite.is_empty()\n1000\t        || !post_local.hash_mismatch.is_empty();\n1001\t\n1002\t    if !needs_rebuild || !still_needs_rebuild {\n1003\t        // Healthy (or locally repaired) — the JSONL rebuild is unreachable from here.\n1004\t        report.healthy = true;\n1005\t        // A clean state clears any prior repeat-failure marker.\n1006\t        let _ = recovery::clear_repair_failed_marker(&beads_dir);\n1007\t        if opts.flush_first && post_local.unflushed_count > 0 && db_path.exists() {\n1008\t            // The user explicitly asked to checkpoint; flushing is a safe, non-rebuild\n1009\t            // operation, so honor it even though no rebuild was needed.\n1010\t            if let Ok(storage) = Storage::open(&db_path) {\n1011\t                if let Ok(flushed) = storage.sync_to_jsonl(&jsonl_path, false) {\n1012\t                    report\n1013\t                        .messages\n1014\t                        .push(format!(\"Flushed {} unflushed bead(s) to JSONL\", flushed));\n1015\t                }\n1016\t            }\n1017\t        } else if post_local.unflushed_count > 0 {\n1018\t            report.messages.push(format!(\n1019\t                \"{} unflushed bead(s) present; run `bf sync --flush-only` to checkpoint them\",\n1020\t                post_local.unflushed_count\n1021\t            ));\n1022\t        }\n1023\t        return Ok(report);\n1024\t    }\n1025\t\n1026\t    // ---- Layer 4: JSONL authority preflight ----\n1027\t    preflight_jsonl(&jsonl_path)?;\n1028\t\n1029\t    // ---- Optional pre-flush, then Layer 6 snapshot ----\n1030\t    if opts.flush_first && db_path.exists() {\n1031\t        // Only possible if the DB is readable; skip silently on a corrupt DB.\n1032\t        if let Ok(storage) = Storage::open(&db_path) {\n1033\t            if let Ok(flushed) = storage.sync_to_jsonl(&jsonl_path, false) {\n1034\t                report\n1035\t                    .messages\n1036\t                    .push(format!(\"Flushed {} bead(s) to JSONL before rebuild\", flushed));\n1037\t            }\n1038\t        }\n1039\t    }\n1040\t\n1041\t    // Snapshot unflushed dirty beads (with labels/deps/comments and any dirty\n1042\t    // tombstones) unless the caller explicitly opted into the destructive path.\n1043\t    let dirty_snapshot: Vec<Issue> = if opts.force {\n1044\t        Vec::new()\n1045\t    } else if db_path.exists() {\n1046\t        Storage::open(&db_path)\n1047\t            .and_then(|s| s.list_dirty_issues())\n1048\t            .unwrap_or_default()\n1049\t    } else {\n1050\t        Vec::new()\n1051\t    };\n1052\t\n1053\t    // ---- Layer 3: verified pre-rebuild backup ----\n1054\t    let manifest = recovery::create_backup(\n1055\t        &beads_dir,\n1056\t        &backup_targets(&db_path, &jsonl_path),\n1057\t        \"pre-rebuild\",\n1058\t    )?;\n1059\t    report.backup_run_id = Some(manifest.run_id.clone());\n1060\t\n1061\t    // ---- Rebuild from JSONL ----\n1062\t    let imported = rebuild_db_from_jsonl(&db_path, &jsonl_path)?;\n1063\t    report.rebuilt = true;\n1064\t    report.imported = imported;\n1065\t\n1066\t    // ---- Layer 6: restore preserved dirty beads ----\n1067\t    report.preserved_dirty = restore_dirty_snapshot(&db_path, &dirty_snapshot)?;\n1068\t\n1069\t    // ---- Post-verification: did the rebuild produce a sound workspace? ----\n1070\t    let verify = check(workspace_dir)?;\n1071\t    // Preserved dirty beads legitimately show up as db-only (missing_in_jsonl); that\n1072\t    // is the expected unflushed state and must not count as a verification failure.\n1073\t    let verify_ok = verify.db_ok\n1074\t        && verify.jsonl_ok\n1075\t        && verify.missing_in_sqlite.is_empty()\n1076\t        && verify.hash_mismatch.is_empty();\n1077\t\n1078\t    if !verify_ok {\n1079\t        // Roll back to the verified backup and raise the repeat-failure gate.\n1080\t        let detail = format!(\n1081\t            \"post-verify failed (db_ok={}, jsonl_ok={}, missing_in_sqlite={}, hash_mismatch={}); \\\n1082\t             restored backup run {}\",\n1083\t            verify.db_ok,\n1084\t            verify.jsonl_ok,\n1085\t            verify.missing_in_sqlite.len(),\n1086\t            verify.hash_mismatch.len(),\n1087\t            manifest.run_id\n1088\t        );\n1089\t        recovery::restore_run(&beads_dir, &manifest.run_id)?;\n1090\t        recovery::write_repair_failed_marker(&beads_dir, &detail)?;\n1091\t        return Err(anyhow!(\n1092\t            \"Rebuild failed post-verification; restored the pre-rebuild backup (run {}). {}. \\\n1093\t             Further rebuilds will refuse without --allow-repeated-repair.\",\n1094\t            manifest.run_id,\n1095\t            detail\n1096\t        ));\n1097\t    }\n1098\t\n1099\t    // Success — clear any prior failure marker.\n1100\t    recovery::clear_repair_failed_marker(&beads_dir)?;\n1101\t    report.messages.push(format!(\n1102\t        \"Rebuilt from JSONL: {} imported, {} unflushed bead(s) preserved; verified backup at {}/{}\",\n1103\t        report.imported,\n1104\t        report.preserved_dirty,\n1105\t        recovery::RECOVERY_DIR,\n1106\t        manifest.run_id\n1107\t    ));\n1108\t\n1109\t    Ok(report)\n1110\t}\n1111\t\n1112\t/// Rebuild the blocked issues cache.\n1113\t///\n1114\t/// This materialized view should be rebuilt after dependency or status changes.\n1115\tpub fn rebuild_cache(workspace_dir: &Path) -> Result<()> {\n1116\t    let beads_dir = find_beads_dir(workspace_dir)\n1117\t        .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!(\"No .beads directory found in {}\", workspace_dir.display()))?;\n1118\t    let metadata = load_metadata(&beads_dir)?;\n1119\t    let db_path = beads_dir.join(&metadata.database);\n1120\t\n1121\t    let storage = Storage::open(&db_path)?;\n1122\t    storage.rebuild_blocked_cache()?;\n1123\t\n1124\t    Ok(())\n1125\t}\n1126\t\n1127\t/// Reclaim stale in_progress beads.\n1128\t///\n1129\t/// Resets beads that have been in_progress for longer than the TTL\n1130\t/// back to open status.\n1131\t///\n1132\t/// # Arguments\n1133\t/// * `workspace_dir` - Path to the workspace root (contains .beads/)\n1134\t/// * `ttl_minutes` - TTL in minutes (default from config is 30)\n1135\t///\n1136\t/// # Returns\n1137\t/// * `Ok(usize)` - Number of beads reclaimed\n1138\tpub fn reclaim_stale(workspace_dir: &Path, ttl_minutes: i64) -> Result<usize> {\n1139\t    let beads_dir = find_beads_dir(workspace_dir)\n1140\t        .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!(\"No .beads directory found in {}\", workspace_dir.display()))?;\n1141\t    let metadata = load_metadata(&beads_dir)?;\n1142\t    let db_path = beads_dir.join(&metadata.database);\n1143\t\n1144\t    let storage = Storage::open(&db_path)?;\n1145\t\n1146\t    let reclaimed = storage.with_immediate_transaction(|tx| {\n1147\t        let stale_cutoff = chrono::Utc::now() - chrono::Duration::minutes(ttl_minutes);\n1148\t\n1149\t        let reclaimed = tx.execute(\n1150\t            \"UPDATE issues\n1151\t             SET status = 'open', assignee = NULL, updated_at = ?\n1152\t             WHERE status = 'in_progress' AND updated_at < ?\",\n1153\t            rusqlite::params![chrono::Utc::now().to_rfc3339(), stale_cutoff.to_rfc3339()],\n1154\t        )?;\n1155\t\n1156\t        Ok::<_, anyhow::Error>(reclaimed)\n1157\t    })?;\n1158\t\n1159\t    Ok(reclaimed)\n1160\t}\n1161\t\n1162\t/// Initialize a new database from an existing JSONL file.\n1163\t///\n1164\t/// Use this to create a fresh database from a JSONL export without\n1165\t/// affecting the existing database.\n1166\t///\n1167\t/// # Arguments\n1168\t/// * `workspace_dir` - Path to the workspace root (contains .beads/)\n1169\t/// * `jsonl_path` - Path to the JSONL file to import from\n1170\t///\n1171\t/// # Returns\n1172\t/// * `Ok(usize)` - Number of beads imported\n1173\tpub fn init_from_jsonl(workspace_dir: &Path, jsonl_path: &Path) -> Result<usize> {\n1174\t    let beads_dir = find_beads_dir(workspace_dir)\n1175\t        .ok_or_else(|| anyhow!(\"No .beads directory found in {}\", workspace_dir.display()))?;\n1176\t    let metadata = load_metadata(&beads_dir)?;\n1177\t    let db_path = beads_dir.join(&metadata.database);\n1178\t\n1179\t    // Check if JSONL exists\n1180\t    if !jsonl_path.exists() {\n1181\t        return Err(anyhow!(\"JSONL file not found at {}\",
